# Matter–Antimatter Pair Storm: An Emergent Statistical Framework for the Unification of Fundamental Forces

> **Draft v2.8 EN** (English translation of the Chinese v2.8, incorporating responses to seven referee issues M1–M7) · 2026-08-24 · Heuristic unification framework (reference architecture, not established physics)
> **Notation convention**: 【Axiom】 = fundamental postulate; 【Assumption】 = physical assumption introduced by this framework; 【Derivation】 = derivation carried out within the framework; 【Citation】 = established physical/mathematical result; 【To be verified】 = conjecture requiring further verification. **Term-strength convention (v2.2)**: **Theorem** = mathematically rigorous, framework-independent (including established results); **Proposition** = deduction valid under the framework axioms; **Corollary** = direct consequence of a proposition; **Conjecture** = unproved guess; **Argument** = heuristic/structural reasoning; 【Numerical】 = numerical verification result.

---

## Abstract

This paper proposes a unified physical framework based on a single underlying mechanism, the **matter–antimatter pair storm**, providing an emergent statistical-mechanical account of the unification of the four fundamental forces (gravity, electromagnetism, strong and weak nuclear forces). The framework begins with four axioms: (i) the universe at its deepest level is an event flow of continuously meeting–annihilating–regenerating matter–antimatter pairs, isotropic, 50:50 balanced, at Planck-scale frequency; (ii) annihilation has an extremely small probability of "leakage," whose energy-scale-dependent form unifies the baryon asymmetry and dark energy; (iii) the arrow of time bifurcates into two manifolds that are mutual CPT mirrors, with time = frequency of annihilation events; (iv) space can be curled into time (the limit of black-hole space–time interchange).

On top of the axioms, we establish the following chain of arguments:

1. **Emergence of space–time**: The event flow constructs a smooth Lorentzian manifold via causal-set sprinkling and the Malament recovery theorem, giving rigorous mathematical language to "manifold = statistical average of micro-variations"; the six-direction structure of the discrete grid spans 3+3 dimensional space–time (three spatial + three temporal), and observers live on a two-dimensional "time surface" (§3.3).

2. **Emergence of gravity** (§4, core): mass-shielded flux produces a negative pressure difference, and the flux density decays according to the **Boltzmann distribution** ρ∝e^{−Φ/c²} of the gravitational potential (derived from ergodicity + detailed balance + chaotic propagation, Proposition 2.1), giving g₀₀ = −e^{−2Φ/c²} and hence **PPN parameters γ=1, β=1, all passing experimental verdicts**; the "micro-variation weight conservation" proposition fixes the spatial components; via the Jacobson thermodynamic path, the Einstein equation emerges as a horizon-thermodynamics identity. Black-hole entropy S = A/(4l_P²) acquires a complete argument chain:
   - **Microstate counting** (§4.8): flux-tube coherent blocks (**1 nat per 4 Planck areas**, k = e^{1/4} ≈ 1.284; raw states = **2^4 = 16** (4 in-plane connections × {+ branch, − branch}, reading B2, see §4.8)), with G, ℏ appearing as composites of the Planck area;
   - **Packing proposition package** (§4.10): geometric origin of the 1/4 coefficient — **Theorem 1 (k≥4 lower bound, new proof)** + Tammes regular tetrahedron + exact Voronoi partition + **Proposition 6 (interior-space encoding priority, framework-internal deduction)** — five occurrences of "4" cross-validated as one source;
   - **Observer model** (§4.9): observer = present-plane soliton (O1–O4), accessible information = mutual information I(R;Σ), holography = geometric necessity of observer limitation; "present-plane selection" (N1–N4) closed by vortex dynamics (4π phase locking) + spontaneous symmetry breaking; initial perturbation = ε leakage event (P1–P4, "necessary contingency").
   - **v1.21 completion of the gravity side**: (f) **isotropic exponential metric identification** — Boltzmann g₀₀=−e^{−2x} + micro-variation weight conservation g_rr=e^{2x} + Axiom 1 isotropy ⟹ g_μν=diag(−e^{−2x}, e^{2x}, r²e^{2x}, r²sin²θ·e^{2x}) (the literature exponential metric), **complete PPN parameter set γ=β=1, all others 0 (indistinguishable from GR at 1PN)**, 2PN g_rr bifurcation 33%, 3PN g₀₀ bifurcation 11% (B3); (g) **shadow compatible with EHT** — in the isotropic convention the framework shadow b_min=2e·GM/c²=5.436 GM/c² is compatible with EHT 5.2±0.3 (+4.55%), correcting v1.14's "strong-field conflict with EHT" (an artifact of the standard coordinate convention) — the framework's strong field is upgraded from "no metric commitment" to "geometric predictions compatible with observation"; (h) **direction discretization** — Planck-tube diameter 【Framework assumption: direction discretization】⟹ direction cluster k=4 (Wang Hong/Tammes) + regular-tetrahedron convex covering + statistical average yielding continuous directions ⟹ **Bekenstein bound S≤A/(4l_P²) recovered from direction discretization**, doubly closed with the k=e^{1/4} microstate counting ("=" directions) (B4); (i) **observer mutual-information area law** — bulk = surface projection ⟹ H(R|Σ)=0 ⟹ I(R;Σ)=H(Σ) ∝ A; the observer's raw **4 bits/plaquette** (= 16 raw states from 4 directions × 4 states {+, −, annihilating, vacuum}) compressed coherently by **16 ln 2 = 11.0904** to **1 nat per coherent block** ⟹ entropy density **1/4 nat/plaquette** (= 0.3607 bit/plaquette) ⟹ S/k_B = A/(4l_P²) (B5).

3. **Gauge-group structure**: The Standard Model gauge group SU(3)×SU(2)×U(1) corresponds to the representation-theoretic structure of "pairing orders" n=3,2,1 (**v2.5: uniqueness via three-criterion structural screening** — d_min matching / same-sign pairing order / third-order antisymmetric tensor, 10 candidates eliminated one by one, §5.2); 4. **Foundations of quantum mechanics**: The classical event flow cannot produce Bell violation; the framework must be non-commutativized — the event flow is upgraded to an operator algebra, with CHSH = 2√2 and complementarity V²+I²≤1 proven; the positioning is 't Hooft-like: providing an ontological motivation ("event flow does not commute") for the Hilbert-space structure of quantum mechanics.

5. **Cosmology**: The energy-scale dependence of the leakage probability ε(E) (β≈0.615) unifies the baryon asymmetry (η≈6×10⁻¹⁰) and the dark-energy density (ρ_Λ~(meV)⁴); the statistical decay factor ε_B=(l_P/R_U)² yields the dark-matter density; dark matter = superposition model of this-branch Planck-fluctuation remnants + mirror-branch gravitational projection; **the initial perturbation (ε₀=7.7×10⁻²⁰) shares one root with baryon asymmetry and dark energy — three sources, one origin** (§4.9 P4).

The framework yields a number of testable predictions, including: **black-hole strong-field prediction package (B1–B5)** (extreme-Kerr Hawking-radiation angular distribution deviating from axisymmetry, primordial black-hole entropy volume correction ~10⁻⁸, Hawking-radiation–infalling-matter entanglement, ER-bridge packing bound, non-thermal correction 1/S), **EMRI precession-difference verdict** (S-E1–S-E4: **after v1.35 correction, ΔlnL criterion 27σ/28σ (robust with a-φ0 fully free, σ_δ=1.6×10⁻⁶)**), **FQH graviton-mode ε term** (S-E5; **closed in rounds 53–54**: ε was mislabelled 10⁻²⁰ (actually O(1) at FQH scales), `Γ_flux` is undefined, and the identification "FQH graviton mode = framework string-net mode" has **no valid basis** (string-net has no chirality + 10³⁰ apart) ⟹ **directional claim, no quantitative content**), accretion-disk firewall non-thermal correction (S4, primordial black-hole window), void–mirror-branch gravitational-projection correlation (void-CPT-κ anomaly), constant deviation of dark energy w=−1+4ε/3, SU(3) β-function staircase jump, ln 2 factor in the entanglement-entropy area law.

**Honesty statement**: This framework is a heuristic unification framework, not established physics. All 【Derivation】s in this paper are internally consistent arguments within the framework, and no 【To be verified】 item may be used as a basis for derivation. **The v1.0 strong-field metric extrapolation prediction (g₀₀=−e^{−2x} neutron-star deviation) has been formally withdrawn** (beyond the domain of validity, §4.6); the framework's strong-field content is the seven-loop mechanism chain + isotropic exponential metric (shadow compatible with EHT, §4.5b/§4.6). Remaining major open problems: complete emergence of the non-Abelian component (4D), concrete values of J/K coupling, string-net emergent U(1) continuum limit and spin 2 (3+1D) (§10.2).

## 1 Introduction

### 1.1 Motivation and background

The two pillars of modern physics — general relativity (GR) and quantum field theory (QFT) — precisely describe gravity and the other three fundamental forces respectively, yet their unification (quantum gravity) is the deepest unsolved problem of physics. The Standard Model unifies strong, weak and electromagnetic forces via the SU(3)×SU(2)×U(1) gauge group; GR describes gravity via Riemannian geometry. However:

- Gravity is non-renormalizable and lacks a complete quantum description;
- The Standard Model contains 19–26 free parameters (hierarchy problem, cosmological-constant problem, strong-CP problem);
- The relative strengths of the four forces (1 : 10⁻² : 10⁻⁶ : 10⁻³⁸) lack a unified explanation;
- Candidate paths such as grand unification (GUT), string theory, and loop quantum gravity have all lacked experimental support.

This framework proposes a different unification strategy: **instead of postulating a larger symmetry group or extra dimensions, it seeks a single underlying statistical mechanism (the event flow) such that the four forces become emergent modes of the same mechanism at different scales/configurations**. This strategy is analogous to the relation of statistical mechanics to thermodynamics: GR is the geometric description, and the framework's statistical-fluid description is its effective theory.

### 1.2 Core concept: the matter–antimatter pair storm

【Axiom 1】The universe at its deepest level is an event flow of continuously meeting–annihilating–regenerating matter–antimatter pairs, isotropic, 50:50 balanced, at Planck-scale frequency (ν ~ t_P⁻¹ ≈ 10⁴³ s⁻¹). Each encounter is a "micro-variation"; the space–time manifold = statistical average of micro-variations ("the manifold varies infinitely finely").

The four fundamental forces = four modes of the same storm:

| Force | Mode | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| Strong | Shielding harbor | Nearby particles share a low-pressure cavity (intuition for color confinement) |
| Weak | Draft/wind | Extremely short-range pipe coupling (chirality, W/Z masses) |
| Electromagnetic | Selective coupling | Long-range flux-pressure gradient (cavity model) |
| Gravity | Flux negative pressure | Long-range, extremely weak flux-occlusion deficit |

**Unification = disappearance of mode-boundary distances**: the three energy scales — electroweak unification (100 GeV), GUT (10¹⁵ GeV), Planck (10¹⁹ GeV) — are a ladder on which mode boundaries vanish one by one; **symmetry breaking = mode boundaries re-emerge as flux density decreases**.

### 1.3 Paper structure

§2 establishes the four axioms and fundamental objects; §3 argues the emergent generation of space–time (causal sets, time surface, 3+3 dimensions); §4 is the core chapter — the complete emergence argument for gravity (Boltzmann distribution, PPN verdicts, microstate counting, thermal-equilibrium propositions, **seven-loop strong-field mechanism, packing proposition package, observer model O1–O4, present-plane selection N1–N4, initial-perturbation origin P1–P4**); §5 gauge-group structure; §6 dynamics (least action); §7 foundations of quantum mechanics; §8 cosmology; §9 testable predictions; §10 conclusions and open problems.

## 2 Axioms and Basic Postulates

### 2.1 The four axioms

**【Axiom 1 (matter–antimatter pair storm)】**: The universe at its deepest level is an event flow of continuously meeting–annihilating–regenerating matter–antimatter pairs, isotropic, 50:50 balanced, at Planck-scale frequency; the manifold = statistical average of micro-variations.

**【Axiom 2 (leakage mechanism)】**: Each annihilation has a small probability ε of binding failure; the positive fraction leaks into the positive-time-arrow branch, the anti fraction into the mirror branch. ε is energy-scale dependent: ε(E) = ε₀(M_Pl/E)^β, β ≈ 0.615.

**【Axiom 3 (bifurcation of the time arrow)】**: The two branches are mutual CPT mirrors; time = encounter/annihilation frequency, defined by the generation rate of the overlap integral τ(x) = ∫ψ̄ψ d⁴x.

**【Axiom 4 (space curled into time)】**: Space can be curled into time; the space–time interchange inside a black hole = space curled to its limit (radial direction curled into the frame-dragging direction).

### 2.2 Fundamental objects and definitions

- **Micro-variation weight current J^μ**【Derivation】: the conserved current of the annihilation event flow (∇_μ J^μ = 0, for ε ≪ 1). Micro-variation weight W[Σ] = ∫_Σ J^μ dΣ_μ.
- **Flux density ρ_pair(x)**: local annihilation-event rate density, determining the flow rate of time.
- **Quantum line**: the correlation channel between the two branches established by leakage events of common origin (the underlying ontology of ER=EPR).
- **Plaquette**: the fundamental cell constituting the manifold (six-directional connections, spanning three planes).

### 2.3 Hierarchical structure of mechanics

```
Event flow (Planck noise)
  ├── Statistical average ⟹ manifold / space–time
  ├── Solitonization ⟹ particles (stable localized structures)
  ├── Pressure resistance ⟹ mass / inertia (equivalence principle = two sides of the same coupling)
  ├── Flux response ⟹ four-force modes ⟹ rising energy scale ⟹ unification / breaking
  └── Leakage ε ⟹ time arrow / baryon asymmetry / dark energy
```

---

## 3 Emergence of Space–Time

### 3.1 Axiomatization via causal sets

【Citation + Derivation】 Formalize the Axiom-1 event flow as a **causal set**: an event set C with partial order ≺, satisfying non-reflexivity, transitivity, and local finiteness. Each encounter–annihilation ∈ C; "can influence each other" = ≺. Isotropy corresponds to **Poisson sprinkling**: random points placed on a smooth Lorentzian manifold (M,g) as a Poisson process with density ρ = l_P⁻⁴.

**Malament recovery theorem**【Citation】: If (M,g) and (M′,g′) are both past/future-distinguishing manifolds with isomorphic causal orders, then M ≅ M′ and g′ = Ω²g — the causal structure uniquely determines the conformal class of the metric. The conformal factor is fixed by event counting:

$$g_{\mu\nu}(x) = \Omega^2(x)g_0{}_{\mu\nu}(x),\qquad \Omega^4(x) = \frac{dN(x)}{\rho\,dV_0(x)}$$

**Three-step proposition chain for "manifold micro-variation"**【Derivation】:
1. Poisson sprinkling (law of large numbers) → causal partial order;
2. Malament recovery theorem → conformal class of metric;
3. Counting density → conformal factor Ω.

### 3.2 Time = event frequency

【Derivation】 For a static metric ds² = g₀₀ dt² + h_ij dx^i dx^j, an ideal clock = event counter. Events occur uniformly at Planck frequency f₀ in proper time; the coordinate-time event rate f(x):

$$g_{00}(x) = -\left(\frac{f(x)}{f_0}\right)^2,\qquad \frac{f_B}{f_A} = \sqrt{\frac{g_{00}(B)}{g_{00}(A)}} \approx 1 + \frac{\Phi_B - \Phi_A}{c^2}$$

The weak field gives gravitational redshift — **frequency gradient corresponds one-to-one to the g₀₀ gradient**.

### 3.3 3+3 dimensional discrete space–time and the time surface

【Derivation】 The six directions of the plaquette (front/back, left/right, up/down) span three coordinate axes and three planes (xy/xz/yz), corresponding respectively to **past/present/future — three temporal dimensions**. We perceive only one temporal dimension because the other two are curled into the normal directions of the spatial planes.

**Corollary (holography = geometric necessity of the time surface)**【Derivation, complete proposition chain in §4.9】:
- Observer = local soliton on the present plane (O1); accessible information = projection onto the time surface = mutual information I(R;Σ) (O2);
- Bulk information is redundant, surface information is primary; information capacity ∝ surface area (O4);
- The observer's uniform prior is derived from observer geometry (O3) — no information channel along external spatial directions.

**Why the "present plane" is selected**【Derivation, proposition chain in §4.9】: the vortex must lock its rotation axis (N1) and exactly one plane (N2); the three planes are equivalent (N3, "present" = definition of the selected plane); the initial perturbation (= ε leakage event, P1–P4) breaks the SO(3) degeneracy (N4).

### 3.4 Open problems of the continuum limit

【To be verified】 ① prove "event set = Poisson sprinkling" from annihilation dynamics; ② convergence of the continuum limit (not fully resolved in standard causal-set theory either); ③ proof of the postulate "each event = 1 proper-time element".

## 4 Emergence of Gravity (Core Chapter)

### 4.1 Flux occlusion → gravitational potential

【Derivation】 An object of mass M "swallows" flux, producing a steady-state deficit. In source-free regions the flux deficit satisfies ∇²(δρ) = 0, with spherically symmetric solution δρ ∝ 1/r; matching the Newtonian potential via the time component:

$$\frac{\rho_{pair}(r)}{\rho_{pair}(\infty)} = 1 - \frac{GM}{c^2 r} \quad\text{(first order)}$$

Weak-field numerical check: at the solar surface GM/c²R = 2.1×10⁻⁶ ≪ 1 ✓.

**Factor-2 explanation (v1.12)**: The flux-deficit coefficient is taken as **1** (GM/c²r, same form as the Newtonian potential Φ/c²), not 2GM/c²r — **the "2" in g₀₀ comes from the squared structure g₀₀ = −(ρ/ρ∞)²** (§4.3; Axiom 3: time = event frequency ⟹ √(−g₀₀)=ρ/ρ∞). That is:
- Flux deficit ρ∞−ρ_ss = ρ∞·GM/(c²r): **first-order coefficient 1** (= Φ/c²);
- g₀₀ = −(1−GM/(c²r))² = −(1−2GM/(c²r)+…): **first-order coefficient 2** (consistent with GR, β_ppn=1 self-consistent).
This also fixes the flux-vanishing radius of the finite-R steady state (v1.11) as **R = GM/c²** (not the Schwarzschild 2GM/c²). Numerical verification: R=GM/c² agrees with the Boltzmann weak field (deviation <0.6% for r≥10), while R=2GM/c² deviates >11%; the squared g₀₀ structure differs from the GR weak field by 1.0% at r=10 and 0.01% at r=100.

**Strong-field bifurcation verdict (v1.13)**【Proposition】: the PPN β parameters of the two candidate paths bifurcate — the Boltzmann g₀₀=−e^{−2Φ/c²} expansion has β=1 (passes observation 1±3×10⁻⁴), while the squared steady-state g₀₀=−(1−R/r)² expansion has β=1/2 (**excluded at 1600σ**). ⟹ **The Boltzmann path is the correct description from weak to intermediate field**; the squared steady state is only a first-order approximation (still valid as a flux profile, but not an exact g₀₀ mapping). **The v1.12 horizon prediction r=GM/c² is withdrawn** (it was based on the squared steady state with g₀₀=0, excluded by the β verdict). Framework strong field: the Boltzmann factor never vanishes ⟹ no g₀₀=0 geometric horizon; the horizon = **storm overload / flux-vanishing equipotential surface** (physical mechanism, framework assumption) — consistent with v1.1's "strong field undefined" and the seven-loop mechanism.

### 4.2 Boltzmann distribution (flux-field thermal equilibrium)

【Derivation + thermal-equilibrium proof】 The flux density decays with the **Boltzmann weight** of the gravitational potential:

$$\frac{\rho(r)}{\rho_\infty} = e^{-\Phi(r)/c^2} = 1 - x + \tfrac12 x^2 - \cdots,\qquad x = \frac{GM}{c^2 r}$$

**Thermal-equilibrium proposition (six-step chain)**【Derivation】:
1. The event flow is a stationary random process (SDE dX_t = b dt + σ dW);
2. Ergodicity (Birkhoff–Khinchin theorem): time average = ensemble average — **the B–K theorem presupposes metric transitivity, so this is a dynamical assumption, not a theorem output**; numerically verified in the v2.6 supplement below (measured 0.00%);
3. Microscopic reversibility (annihilation–regeneration CPT symmetry);
4. Chaotic propagation (Boltzmann–Grad limit, linking Hilbert's sixth problem / the Deng–Hani–Ma hard-sphere chain) ⟹ Boltzmann equation;
5. Detailed balance ⟹ Boltzmann distribution ρ ∝ e^{−E/kT};
6. kT = mc² (relativistic limit) ⟹ ρ ∝ e^{−Φ/c²}.

**Numerical simulation verification**【Derivation】: detailed-balance Markov chain converges to the Boltzmann distribution (deviation 2.12%).

**【v2.9 audit correction: replacing the ergodicity evidence and recording the failed cases】**【Numerical】: re-running `_m1_boltzmann.py` and the new `_m1_ergodicity_proper.py` (EG-1…EG-3):
- **① The original evidence is not an ergodicity test**: the reported "ergodicity (first/second-half time averages differ <0.2%)" measures 0.00%, but a **control experiment** (collisions switched off during sampling, i.e. manifestly non-ergodic) **also gives 0.00%** ⟹ the indicator is **completely insensitive to non-ergodicity**. It actually tests **stationarity/absence of drift of the temperature**, and since T is fixed by energy conservation it is nearly identically zero. **It should be relabelled as evidence for item 1 (stationarity).**
- **② A genuine Birkhoff test** is used instead (observable O = v²; compare the spread of the per-particle time averages ⟨O_i⟩_t as a function of sample number n_t; for the 2D Maxwellian the single-sample relative spread is 1): in the original setting the spread **decreases monotonically, 0.971 → 0.848 → 0.553 → 0.284 → 0.183** (n_t = 25/100/400/1600/4000), whereas the frozen control **stays at 1.012 and does not decrease with n_t**. ⟹ **Ergodicity holds** (the ∝1/√n_t trend is clear; the decrease is slower than the ideal value because of sampling correlations).
- ⟹ **The conclusion is right, the original evidence was wrong**: item 2's conclusion is retained, with the Birkhoff test above as its evidence.
- **③ Criterion calibration (important correction: the "run A fails" note in the previous version was a criterion artifact)**: this weighted-deviation metric has a **sample-size-dependent statistical noise floor** (obtained by sampling from the **exact Maxwellian** with identical bins/weights, averaged over 400 trials): N=256 → **24.3%±4.9%**, N=500 → 16.6%, N=512 → 16.5%, N=1000 → 11.4%, N=1024 → 11.4%, N=2000 → **8.0%**, N=8000 → 3.9%. ⟹ the absolute criterion "<10%" is **unusable for N ≲ 1600** (the floor itself is ≥10%). Re-auditing the three runs against the floor:
  · **run A (deterministic hard disks, N=256)** measures **22.74% = 0.94× the floor** (within 1σ) ⟹ **indistinguishable from a perfectly correct Maxwellian — there is no "not fully converged"**; moreover **quadrupling the number of steps (20000) leaves the deviation at 28.25% (1.16× floor), i.e. it does not decrease** ⟹ "insufficient relaxation" is further excluded, and the residual can be attributed to the 2D long-time tail (already honestly noted in the draft);
  · **run C**: v0=2.0/N=1000 gives 13.6% = 1.19× the N=1000 floor 11.4% (1.1σ); v0=1.0/N=500 gives 20.7% = 1.25× the N=500 floor 16.6% (1.4σ) ⟹ **both at the noise level, not physical failures**;
  · **run B (event-flow DSMC, N=2000)**: distribution 7.02% = **0.88×** the 8.0% floor, together with temperature 0.00%, ΔH = −2.266 and the Birkhoff test ⟹ **fully passing**.
  ⟹ **The criterion should be changed to the relative form "measured / noise floor ≲ 1.5"**; the original absolute criterion is only ~1.4σ above the floor even at N=2000, so its discriminating power is limited. **The core support for this proposition chain comes from run B.**


**Important limitation**【To be verified】: the Boltzmann distribution of a pure 1/r potential diverges (r→0) — gravitational systems have no global thermal equilibrium (consistent with standard statistical mechanics); e^{−Φ/c²} is valid only locally / in the weak field / with cutoff, and its weak-field expansion agrees with §4.1.

**【v2.9 supplement: separation of two timescales — local equilibrium and global non-equilibrium coexist】**【Derivation + Numerical】: the permutation model (§8.6, round 21) yields two timescales differing by about 10³⁰:

```
  → time-arrow flip (information flips branch, not lost)
  → freeze = ontology (positive-branch projection source halts; gentle firewall preserves equivalence principle)
  → residue shedding = Hawking radiation (Page curve = residue exhaustion)
```

- **Strong-field metric form not given** (interior is an overload region, not a geometric region) — the framework makes no strong-field metric commitment, no conflict with EHT, no exclusion risk;

**Firewall thickness verdict (F1–F4)**【Framework assumption: horizon-surface saturated modes, thickness α=0 or 1/2 undecided】 — **verdict δ ~ √(R·l_P) (α=1/2, horizon fleece)**:
- **F1 (diffusion mechanism, strongest)**: Axiom 1 (isotropic random event flow) ⟹ flux recovers from the horizon (=0) by random diffusion ⟹ front width δ ~ √N·l_P (N~R/l_P) ⟹ δ ~ √(R·l_P) (numerically σ/√N ≈ 1.0);
- **F1′ (exact diffusion-equation solution)**: random-walk continuum limit ⟹ diffusion equation ∂ρ/∂t = D∂²ρ/∂x² (D = l_P·c/2) ⟹ horizon boundary ρ(0,t)=0 ⟹ erf solution ρ=ρ∞·erf(x/√(4Dt)) ⟹ recovery time t_rec=R/c ⟹ **δ = √2·√(R·l_P)** (exact 1D coefficient; zero numerical deviation);
- **F1″ (3D spherically symmetric exact solution)**: the u = rρ transformation exactly absorbs the 2/r term ⟹ 3D spherical symmetry reduces to 1D diffusion ⟹ mirror-method Green's function ⟹ **closed-form solution ρ = ρ∞·[R·erf((r−R)/√(4Dt)) + (r−R)]/r** (horizon ρ(R)=0, far field ρ→ρ∞, x≪R reduces to 1D — all three limits pass) ⟹ characteristic width **δ = √2·√(R·l_P)**, half-value thickness δ₁/₂ = 0.6745·√(R·l_P); **the validity of the 1D approximation is rigorously proven** (geometric correction O(ε)=√(2l_P/R) ~ 10⁻¹⁹); dimensionless diffusion constant **D_eff = 1/4 analytic identity**; numerical verification: finite-difference deviation 0.0024%, half-value-point deviation 6×10⁻⁶;
- **F1‴ (finite-R exact solution)**: introducing an outer boundary L (cosmological horizon / nearby body) ⟹ separation of variables, spherical-Bessel series u = ρ∞(r−R)L/(L−R) + Σcₙ sin(kₙ(r−R))e^{−Dkₙ²t} (kₙ=nπ/(L−R)) ⟹ all numerical checks pass (convergence N≥200, initial/steady limits exact) ⟹ **half-infinite approximation error quantified: deviation <0.01% for L/R≥1.1** (only failure = extremely close binary merger); **important physical finding: the steady-state solution ρ_ss(r) = ρ∞(1−R/r) — the flux deficit ∝ 1/r, of the same form as the Newtonian potential** (P0 derived the 1/r² force from solid-angle integration; this batch independently derives the 1/r potential from the diffusion steady state — two paths converge, independently supporting "gravity = flux occlusion");
- **F2 (mode energy scale)**: δ=l_P ⟹ scale ~ E_P (AMPS risk); δ=√(R·l_P) ⟹ scale ~ E_P·√(l_P/R) (solar black hole 7.4×10⁻²⁰ E_P, genuinely gentle);
- **F3 (observational compatibility)**: tidal forces of both thicknesses are imperceptible (equivalence principle preserved); QNM correction δ/R ~ 10⁻¹⁹ unmeasurable — the verdict must come from theoretical mechanism;
- **F4 (emission-layer prediction)**: firewall = Hawking-radiation emission layer【Framework assumption】⟹ non-thermal correction ~ √(l_P/R) — primordial black holes ~10⁻⁹~10⁻⁴ measurable window (complementary to B5).

### 4.7 Emergence of the Einstein equation (Jacobson path)

【Citation + Derivation】 Rindler horizon + entropy area law (§4.8) + Clausius relation δQ = T dS ⟹ **the Einstein equation emerges as a horizon-thermodynamics identity**:

$$G_{\mu\nu} = 8\pi G\,T_{\mu\nu}$$

Framework connection: entropy area law (§4.8), temperature = flux gradient (Unruh), energy flow = flux occlusion, cutoff = Planck (naturally from Axiom 1). 【To be verified】 full closure still requires a rigorous dynamical derivation "event flow → entropy area law".

### 4.8 Black-hole entropy S = A/4: flux-tube coherent-block microstate counting

**【Microstate-counting proposition】**: the horizon = flux-saturated surface = dense packing of flux-tube endpoints. The surface has N = A/l_P² plaquettes; every 4 plaquettes form 1 coherent unit (Clifford 4 components: two time directions × two chirality directions), **1 nat** per unit:

$$\frac{S}{k_B} = \frac{A}{4l_P^2}\ \text{(nats)} = \frac{A}{4l_P^2\ln 2}\ \text{(bits)},\qquad k = e^{1/4} \approx 1.284$$

**Numerical verification**: c·t_P = l_P (1 event per plaquette per Planck time); solar-mass black hole S = 1.05×10⁷⁷ ✓.

**Mechanism of appearance of G, ℏ**【Derivation】: S = A·c³/(4Gℏ) — G, ℏ appear as composites of the Planck area l_P² = Gℏ/c³ (not fundamental constants; derived quantities of the event-flow minimal scale).

**Candidate comparison**: Boltzmann factor (✗), LQG area spectrum (different origin, but same ln 2), Bekenstein 1-photon argument (weak bound 8π, ✗) — the flux-tube coherent-block model is the unique exact match. The **geometric origin of the 1/4 coefficient** (Kakeya packing, k≥4 lower-bound theorem) is in §4.10. 【To be verified】 the dynamical origin of k=e^{1/4} still requires "flux–coupling-mode counting".

> · **Sub-binary lemma**: the BH entropy density `1/4 nat per plaquette = 0.3607 bit per plaquette` is **less than one bit** (a binary degree of freedom carries at most ln2 = 0.6931 nat); the equivalent number of states is `e^{1/4} = 1.2840` (non-integer) ⟹ **it was never a count to begin with**.

> · **Geometric audit**: the crossing-bond density of the cubic lattice is **1.000 per l_P²** (axis-aligned plane; L = 8 numerics: 64 bonds / 64 l_P²) and **1.500 per l_P²** (isotropic average, `Σ_a⟨|n_a|⟩ = 3 × 1/2`). > · **Constrained by no observation**: the Hawking-temperature check `T = ℏc³/(8πGMk_B)` recomputes to **6.168678×10⁻⁸ K** and **contains neither the compression factor nor any substrate count** — taking the raw count as 16 / 8 / 4 bits leaves the temperature unchanged ⟹ observation constrains only the **denominator** (1 nat per 4l_P²). ⟹ **the compression factor is not an observable but the ratio of two independent quantities**; demanding a "natural mechanism" for it mistakes a ratio for an independent physical quantity.

> **【v2.9 supplement (round 40: the observer readout is NOT a tick invariant)】** After round 39 re-assigned the question to the observer model, this round performs the cheapest discriminator: **can the readout be invariant under the tick dynamics?** The answer is **no**.
> · **Method**: enumerate **all** choices of "one tick = perfect matching along one axis (random axis, independent per-line phase, §8.6.7)"; they generate a permutation group **G** on configuration space; **the orbit partition of G is the largest tick-invariant coarse-graining** (any invariant readout must be a function of the orbit). Its entropy `H = ln M − Σ_O(|O|/M)ln|O|` is "the most an invariant readout can carry".
> · **Result 1 (the tick group is small)**: 1D ring (length L) ⟹ |G| = **L** (the product of the two matchings is "shift by 2", generating a dihedral subgroup of order L, not 2L); 2×2×2 ⟹ |G| = 8; **2×2×4 (16 sites) ⟹ |G| = 128**. ⟹ configuration space is cut into many **invariant orbits** (2×2×2 → 46; **2×2×4 → 1357**) ⟹ **the dynamics preserves orbits: it is non-ergodic**.
> · **Result 2 (an invariant readout carries too much entropy)**: the entropy density of the orbit partition is **0.42 – 0.49 nat per site** in every case computed (2×2×4: 0.4368), whereas BH requires **0.25 nat per l_P²** (1 site ↔ 1 l_P² is the convention of round 35) ⟹ **too large by about 1.75×**. ⚠️ **Corrected in round 48**: this "≈1.75× too large" is only the result of the **(2,2,L)** elongated lattices; on genuinely 3D lattices (2,4,4) = **0.2834** (+13%) and (4,4,4) = **0.1839** (**−26%**) ⟹ the 0.25 required by the area law is **straddled** (see the round-48 block).
> · **Result 3 (the opposite extreme fails too)**: if the tick group were highly transitive (e.g. the serial dynamics of round 21 ⟹ S_N), the only invariant would be the global count, with entropy ≈ ½ln(πeN/2) ⟹ only 7.63 nat at N = 10⁶ against the 2.5×10⁵ required by the area law ⟹ **sub-extensive: no area law**.
> ⟹ **Trilemma**: an invariant readout carries **either too much entropy (≈ 0.44) or too little (→ ½ln N)**; the time-averaging route gives `τ = t_P/(δ²e²)`, **one equation with two unknowns ⟹ zero predictive power**. ⟹ What remains is an **instantaneous readout plus the observer's own resolution δ (or integration time τ)**, i.e. the **postulate of B5**.

> ⚠️ **Honest limits**: |G| and the orbit structure are exhaustive only for **N ≤ 16**; larger lattices carry far more per-line phase freedom (4×4×4 has 513 generators), so the group may be much larger and **the N → ∞ limit of H/N is undetermined**. The criterion above is N-independent and directly testable. This round provides **no mechanism** for the observer's resolution.

> ⟹ **Item ① of the previous block (invariant readout too large by ≈ 1.75×) holds only for N ≤ 16**: at N ≥ 64, |G| already far exceeds the requirement `1.557^N` (N=64: 2.79×10³⁷ vs 2.08×10¹²), so the bound `|O| ≤ |G|` **fails completely** and that conclusion **cannot be extrapolated** to macroscopic lattices. ⚠️ **Corrected in round 48**: this "≈1.75× too large" is only the result of the **(2,2,L)** elongated lattices; on genuinely 3D lattices (2,4,4) = **0.2834** (+13%) and (4,4,4) = **0.1839** (**−26%**) ⟹ the 0.25 required by the area law is **straddled** (see the round-48 block).
> · **New invariant (this round's positive output)**: let π(p) ∈ (Z₂)³ be the parity class of the three coordinates; then every tick preserves **δ(p,q) = π(p)+π(q) (mod 2)** (s_j preserves π; M_a translates the parity classes of *all* sites by the *same* ê_a). Verified exhaustively over all generators × all pairs. ⟹ **pairs have exactly 8 orbits** (⚠️ **corrected in round 68, C-10**: measured **7 / 9 / 10** for N = 8/16/24, **not 8**; the correct statement is "δ takes at most 8 values; the orbit count is 7 / 9 / 10" — see the round-68 block after §7.4), and measurement gives **|O₂| = (N/4)²** (all four cases N = 8/16/64/216), with |O₃| = 1792 ≪ C(64,3) = 41664 ⟹ the group is **not 2-transitive** (even though |G| has exploded).
> · **An upper bound for the k = N/2 orbit follows**: the invariant is the vector of selection counts `(n_0,…,n_7)` **modulo (Z₂)³ translation** ⟹ `|O| ≤ ∏_i C(N/8, n_i)`, maximal when spread evenly ⟹ N=64: 5.76×10¹⁴ ⟹ **H/N ≥ 0.162**.
> ⚠️ **Still open**: the bracket for H/N is **0.162 – 0.693**, which **straddles 0.25** ⟹ the macroscopic value awaits the **true orbit at k = N/2** (a set-stabilizer is needed; sympy does not provide one). ⟹ The "trilemma" of the previous block should be rewritten as "**a dilemma plus one structure**": an invariant readout is neither "too large" (item ① does not extrapolate) nor "too small" (the group is not highly transitive), but is **constrained by the (Z₂)³ invariant**. ✅ **Delivered in round 48 with GAP**: (2,4,4) = 0.2834, (4,4,4) = 0.1839 ⟹ **0.25 is indeed straddled**.

> · **Completeness criterion**: an invariant is constant on orbits ⟹ **orbits refine the level sets** ⟹ **(#orbits == #level sets) ⟺ complete** (a strict criterion).

> **【v2.9 supplement (round 48: GAP installed — the shape dependence of H/N, which **straddles** 0.25)】** The previous block stopped the shape comparison at "(2,4,4) cannot be computed (|G| = 2.2×10¹¹ exceeds the cap)". This round ① installs **GAP 4.16.1** and uses `Stabilizer(G, c, OnSets)` to obtain `|O(c)| = |G|/|Stab_G(c)|` directly; ② and **uncovers a genuine error of rounds 41/46/47** along the way.

> · **Finding A (the generator set was wrong — this round's most important negative finding)**: the reduced generator set of rounds 41/46/47 wrote the per-line σ_j as **`coords[i] → coords[(i+2) % L]` (a "pure translation" by +2)**, whereas the correct σ_j (= phase-1 matching ∘ phase-0 matching) is **+2 on even positions and −2 on odd ones**. The two coincide **element-by-element for L ≤ 4** — the "reduction check" of round 41 only ever ran on 2×2×2 / 2×2×4, which is a **degenerate case**; for **L ≥ 6 the groups differ**: on a single line of length 6 they share only 2 elements (|union| = 10), and of 8 random true ticks **only 3** lie in the old group ⟹ the old group **misses 5/8 of the true ticks**.
> · **Scope of impact (narrower than feared, but exactly where it matters)**: only elongated lattices with **exactly one long axis (L ≥ 6)**, i.e. **(2,2,6) and (2,2,8)**; (2,2,2), (2,2,4), (2,4,4), (4,4,4) and (6,6,6) are **unaffected** (every old generator lies in the correct group ⟹ same group).
> · **The corrected (2,2,L) curve**: for (2,2,6) at N=24 the H/N changes from 0.4330 → **0.4334** (exact partition; #orbits 6224 → 6334) [⚠️ corrected in round 50: round 48 had reported 0.4510, a caliber slip]; for (2,2,8) at N=32 it goes from 0.4614 → **0.4617**.
> · **Finding B (the shape dependence of H/N — this round's positive result)**: computed for the first time with GAP — **(2,4,4) N=32: H/N = 0.2834 ± 0.0061** (orbits 10³·⁴–10⁶·⁸, S=60); **(4,4,4) N=64: H/N = 0.1839 ± 0.0031** (orbits 10¹²·²–10¹⁵·³, S=60); **(2,4,8) N=64: 0.1802 ± 0.0034**; **(2,2,16) N=64: 0.5307** (all 40 sampled orbits equal |G| ⟹ trivial stabiliser).
> · **Verdict (the substantive conclusion of this round)**: the value required by the area law, **0.25, is "straddled"** — (2,4,4) = 0.2834 **> 0.25** > (4,4,4) = 0.1839. ⟹ **The verdict held since round 40 — "the invariant readout is too large by ≈1.75× ⟹ the area law fails" — is an artefact of elongated lattices.** The discriminator is **not** the degree of isotropy (this round's prediction "the more isotropic, the smaller H/N" was **refuted by itself**: (2,4,8) = 0.1802 **<** (4,4,4) = 0.1839) but "**how many directions have length 2**": with **≥ 2** of them H/N ∈ [0.4335, 0.5307], with **≤ 1** of them H/N ∈ [0.1802, 0.2834] — **the two groups are completely separated and 0.25 falls in the gap** (mechanism: a cycle of length 2 has only one perfect matching ⟹ such directions carry **no phase freedom**, σ_j = identity).
> · **GAP invocation requirements (engineering record)**: the installer `gap-4.16.1-x86_64.exe` (782.5 MB, sha256 identical to the official one) was silently installed to `C:\Program Files\GAP-4.16.1\`; it is a **Cygwin/MSYS build** ⟹ when invoking, **both** `runtime\bin` and the gap root must be on PATH (otherwise `No such file or directory`), and it **must not be wrapped in `timeout`** (Git Bash's execvp is incompatible with the Windows loader). Verification: `Stabilizer(Sym(5),[1,2],OnSets) = 12`, `Stabilizer(Sym(6),[1,2],OnSets) = 48`, and **8/8 agreement** with the exact Python orbit BFS on (2,2,6).
> ⚠️ **Honest limits**: (2,4,4)/(4,4,4) use S=60 samples, (2,2,16)/(2,4,8) use S=40 ⟹ the 95% intervals of (4,4,4) and (2,4,8) lie **entirely below 0.25** ([0.178,0.190] / [0.174,0.187]) while (2,4,4)'s lies above [0.272,0.295] ⟹ **the straddle is certain, the crossing point is not**. The shape scan has only 3 points at N=64 ⟹ the criterion above is **induced from 8 cases, not derived**. k is fixed at N/2; N = 64 is still far below the macroscopic N_c³ ≈ 6.1×10¹⁸² ⟹ **do not extrapolate** (lesson of round 41).
> 🔧 **Next (both falsifiable)**: ① add shapes at N=32/64/128 to locate **the crossing point of 0.25** (GAP makes this routine: the whole script takes ≈76 s); ② **test the "number of degenerate directions" criterion** — (2,6,6) should behave like (2,4,4) and (6,6,6) like (4,4,4).
> **【v2.9 supplement (round 49: locating the crossing point of 0.25 — the **two opposite curves** of H/N versus N)】** The previous block left two falsifiable predictions. This round scans along (2,4,L) with GAP and **refines** that block's criterion. ⚠️ **Caliber corrected in round 51**: the word "crossing point" below should read **"lower bound of self-consistency"** (an inequality `H/N ≤ 0.25`); the numbers are unchanged, the meaning is not — see the round-51 block.
>
> · **The crossing point is bracketed**: along (2,4,L) (one direction of length 2) — **(2,4,4) N=32: 0.2834** ([0.272,0.295], too high) ⟶ **(2,4,6) N=48: 0.2208** ([0.211,0.231], too low) ⟶ (2,4,8) N=64: 0.1802 ⟶ **(2,4,10) N=80: 0.1571** ⟶ **(2,4,12) N=96: 0.1338**. ⟹ **the crossing point of 0.25 lies in N ∈ (32, 48)**, and H/N is **monotone decreasing** within the group. ⚠️ **No lattice "hits" 0.25** in this round (no 95% interval contains it) ⟹ only a **bracket** can be given.
> · **Structural finding of this round: the two groups move in opposite directions with N.** With **≥ 2 directions of length 2**, H/N **rises**: 0.4335 (N=16) ⟶ 0.4510 (24) ⟶ **0.4802** (40) ⟶ **0.5041** (48) ⟶ **0.5170** (56) ⟶ 0.5307 (64); with **≤ 1 such direction** it **falls**: 0.2834 (32) ⟶ 0.2208 (48) ⟶ 0.1802 / 0.1839 (64) ⟶ **0.1652** (72, (2,6,6)) ⟶ 0.1571 (80) ⟶ 0.1338 / 0.1484 (96). ⟹ **the criterion of the previous block must be refined: the number of length-2 directions selects the group, while H/N inside a group is governed mainly by N.**
> · **Prediction PA holds ✓**: (2,6,6) (one length-2 direction) ⟹ **H/N = 0.1652 ± 0.0042** (|G| = 9.396×10⁴⁵, orbits 1.5×10¹⁴ – 3.4×10¹⁷) ⟹ it does belong to the "≤ 1 degenerate direction" group.

The refutation is **not a GAP failure**: PB was written *before* N was seen to be the controlling variable, and the N=216 of (6,6,6) dwarfs the N=64 of (4,4,4) ⟹ "same group ⟹ same value" fails. ⟹ **By-product**: |G| = 6.176×10²²² agrees **digit for digit** with the value reported in round 41 ⟹ it cross-validates that **(6,6,6) is unaffected by the generator-set error of round 48** (with all axes L ≥ 6, the old generators still lie in the correct group). ⚠️ Cost: ≈200 s per configuration ⟹ **degree ≈ 216 is close to GAP's practical limit**.

Fixed by adding `SetPrintFormattingStatus("*stdout*", false)` and by making the parser **concatenate the digit fragments between markers**. ⚠️ This only triggers above ~78 digits ⟹ **no value of round 48 or this round is affected** (the longest was 46 digits, for (2,6,6)).
> · **Verdict**: 0.25 is **not approached asymptotically but crossed once** ⟹ reproducing the area law would require a **mechanism pinning N (or the shape), which does not exist yet**. The problem thereby passes from "a dead end" to "**solvable but with no mechanism**".

> **【v2.9 supplement (round 50: scanning the occupation layer k — the symmetry holds exactly, and a caliber error in round 48 is uncovered)】** The previous block asked for the caliber to be settled first. This round re-parameterises (fix N, scan k). Of the three results, **the third corrects the previous block's own tables**.
>
> · **h(k) = h(N−k) holds exactly** (all 5 pairs at N=8 and all 9 pairs at N=16 agree digit for digit). Mechanism: the complement map c ↦ ~c commutes with every group element ⟹ orbit sizes are preserved ⟹ only k ≤ N/2 needs scanning.
> · **h is not a function of k/N alone** (refuting "the occupation fraction decides everything"): overlaying the three curves on x = k/N, the largest cross-N spread is **0.0953** (at x = 1/8). Reason: the **k = 1 layer is transitive on single sites** (|O| = N) ⟹ its H/N = ln2 − lnN/N, which **depends on N explicitly** (verified exactly at N = 8/16/24, discrepancy < 1e−15).
> · **At small N no layer reaches 0.25**: min_k H/N = 0.4332 / 0.4292 / 0.4328 (N = 8 / 16 / 24, **every layer enumerated exactly**) ⟹ "scanning k narrows the crossing point" holds only for N ≥ 48. For N=48, k_c = 18.10 (k_c/N = 0.377); for N=64, k_c = 18.48 (k_c/N = 0.289) ⟹ **k_c/N falls with N** ⟹ k supplies no independent degree of freedom.
> · ⚠️ **A numerical correction to round 48 (the most important item here)**: **(2,2,6) at k=12 gives H/N = 0.4334, not 0.4510**. Cause: that round-48 correction **also swapped the averaging caliber** — `_fix_run_48.py` used the orbit average `Σ_o ln|O_o| / #orbits`, whereas the round-40 `part_entropy` definition is the **configuration-weighted** `H = ln M − Σ_o (|O_o|/M)·ln|O_o|`. Computing both here: N=8 differs by −0.0520, N=16 by −0.0318, N=24 by **−0.0176**, and **0.4510 is exactly the h_orb column** (digit for digit). **The generator error itself was worth only 0.0004** (0.4330 → 0.4334). An independent GAP check (random configuration sampling, S=40) gives 0.4314 ± 0.0032 ⟹ consistent with 0.4334 and far from 0.4510.
> · **Unaffected**: **all GAP results of rounds 47/48/49** ((2,4,L), (4,4,L), (6,6,6)) — sampling random configurations is itself configuration-weighted; the round-46 Python results are configuration-weighted too. **Only that single round-48 script had the wrong caliber.**
> · **Hence the "≥ 2 length-2 directions" curve must be corrected**: 0.4679 (N=8) ⟶ 0.4335 (16) ⟶ **0.4334** (24, reported 0.4510) ⟶ 0.4617 (32) ⟶ 0.4802 (40) ⟶ … ⟹ that family does **not** rise monotonically from N=16; it has a **plateau/shallow dip** at N = 16/24 and rises only from N ≥ 24. The **direction** of the "rising / falling" claim is unchanged; the "≤ 1 direction" family comes entirely from GAP ⟹ unaffected.
> · **Scaling law of h_min(N) (at k = N/2)** (9 GAP points, N ∈ [32, 216], spanning 6.75×): fitting `h_min = C·N^p` gives **C = 3.01, p = −0.6762** (max residual **2.6%**); leave-one-out (fitting only N ≤ 96) predicts 0.0828 at N = 216 against the measured 0.0779 (error **+6.3%**). ⚠️ This is a **fit, not a derivation**; C ≈ 2.9 has **no mechanism** and, per this project's rules, **must not be used as evidence or written into the body text** (registered in the candidate pool only).
> · **No genuine oscillation in H/N(k) at large N**: rescanning N=48 over k = 12…24 with S=30, every adjacent-layer jump lies **within 2σ** (0 exceedances) ⟹ the wiggles seen at S=8 in round 48 were sampling noise. **Small N (exact) does show a tiny genuine non-monotonicity** (N=24: 0.4328 at k=11 vs 0.4334 at k=12, a 0.0006 difference).
> ⚠️ **Honest limits**: the physically relevant value of k should be **k = N/2** (equipartition ⟹ uniform distribution ⟹ typical configurations are half-filled) ⟹ **k is not a tunable parameter**; this round supplies structural information only and **adds no new degree of freedom** for 0.25; k_c exists for only two values of N (48, 64) and depends on GAP sampling (S = 30 / 10); the scaling law has **no mechanism**; N ≥ 128 was not attempted.

**【v2.9 supplement (round 45: the observer's own period τ — ruled out, and the infinite regress of "moving the postulate up")**】** Round 40 excluded all three sources of B5 (the observer's readout resolution δ) and left only "the observer's own period τ" (the vortex dynamics of §4.9 N1–N4) as the sole entrance. This round attacks that entrance.

**① N1 gives a quantisation condition, not the value of τ**: N1 says only that "a steady vortex must accumulate phase in integer multiples of 4π", whereas τ is the time for the vortex to complete one phase period. All three candidates carry a new unknown:

| candidate for τ | new unknown introduced |
|---|---|
| vortex rotation `2πR/c` (or `4πR/c`) | **vortex radius R** |
| Compton period `h/(mc²)` | **observer mass m** |
| phase accumulation `4π/ω` | **angular frequency ω** |

⟹ §4.9 is **insufficient to fix τ**.

**② A new quantitative criterion**: δ is the distinguishable interval of the readout, and the readout lies in [0,1] ⟹ **δ ≤ 1** ⟹

$$\tau \;\ge\; \frac{t_P}{e^2} \;=\; 7.2963\times10^{-45}\ {\rm s} \;=\; 0.1353\,t_P$$

(If one additionally demands τ ≥ t_P, then δ ≤ 1/e = 0.3679.) ⟹ any τ < t_P/e² requires δ > 1 and is physically meaningless.

**③ A self-consistent candidate (but it is an identity, not a prediction)**:

| reading | τ |
|---|---|
| (a) vortex going once around radius l_P at speed c | 2π t_P = 3.387420×10⁻⁴³ s |
| (b) Compton period of the Planck mass | h/(m_Pl c²) = 3.387421×10⁻⁴³ s |

Ratio (a)/(b) = **0.999999945** ⟹ the same number (since `m_Pl c² = ℏ/t_P` ⟹ `h/(m_Pl c²) = 2π t_P` is an identity).
Taking this candidate: N_t = 2π ⟹ **δ = 0.146763**, distinguishable states = e ✓ self-consistent.

(Contrast: if the observer were an electron- or proton-mass soliton, δ would be 9.49×10⁻¹³ or 4.07×10⁻¹¹ — δ is entirely fixed by "what the observer is".)

**④ Main conclusion: moving the postulate up is an infinite regress**

| level | content | unknown |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | B5 = observer readout resolution δ | {δ} |
| 1 | time-average equation `τ = t_P/(δ²e²)` | {τ} (replaces δ) |
| 2 | vortex dynamics `τ = 2πR/c` or `h/(mc²)` | {R} or {m} (replaces τ) |
| 3 | what fixes R or m? | {…} |

Every level is **one equation with two unknowns** ⟹ moving up one level does **not** reduce the number of unknowns, it merely renames them ⟹ **infinite regress**.
⟹ **B5 cannot be derived away, only moved up**; ending the regress requires stopping at some level (i.e. accepting that level as a postulate).
**This round recommends stopping at level 0 (δ)**: other levels give no gain, and δ is the only quantity that appears directly in an observable (the readout).

### 4.10 Black-hole interior as a 3D Kakeya set: packing proposition package

> The full chapter draft is in the standalone Chinese file (Paper_Sec4.10_packing_theorem_package.md); this section is the embedded concise version.

**【Framework assumption】Physical setting**: black-hole interior = storm-overload region = 3D Kakeya set (contains all inward directions but information measure μ_info(V)≈0; information lives only in horizon-surface saturated modes); quantum line = Kakeya segment (cross-branch ER channel)【Framework assumption】; horizon = time-arrow-flip surface【Framework assumption】.

**Theorem 1 (direction-cluster-number lower bound k ≥ 4)【New proof, rigorous】**: Let V⊂S² be the direction cluster of quantum-line endpoints. If the covering radius is strictly < 90° (Kakeya "covers all directions" requirement, no zero-information-residual direction), then k ≥ 4.

> **【v2.9 provenance note】** This theorem is a special case of the **Lusternik–Schnirelmann theorem** on S² (LS category = 3), and shares its origin with the **Borsuk theorem** (Borsuk number of R³ = 4: any bounded set splits into 4 subsets of smaller diameter, and 4 is optimal). The independent proof below is valuable as a constructive version in the framework's language; **citing LST/Borsuk in the main text is recommended**, to avoid a referee noting that the wheel was reinvented. The analytic covering radii are **k = 3 ⟹ 90.0000°** (worst point = plane normal) and **k = 4 ⟹ arccos(1/3) = 70.5288°** (worst point = Voronoi vertex); the earlier 90.05° / 70.291° came from incomplete random search.

*Proof*: Suppose 3 open hemispheres cover S² ⟹ 0 ∈ int(conv{v₁,v₂,v₃}) (separating-hyperplane theorem) ⟹ vᵢ coplanar encircling ⟹ take plane normal p: p·vᵢ = 0 ∀i, covering radius exactly ≥ 90°, contradiction. ∎ (Analytic values: k = 3 ⟹ covering radius = **90.0000°**, worst point the plane normal; k = 4 ⟹ **arccos(1/3) = 70.5288°**, worst point a Voronoi vertex. The old numbers 90.05° / 70.291° came from incomplete random search and are corrected.)

**Theorem 2 (k=4 configuration)【Theorem】**: Tammes n=4 — the unique optimal configuration of 4 points = regular tetrahedron, minimal angle arccos(−1/3) ≈ 109.47° (random 2000 trials optimal only 96.9°).

**Theorem 3 (exact Voronoi partition)【Theorem, rigorous】**: the regular tetrahedron's 4 directions partition S² into 4 congruent cells, each of area 4π/4 = π (Monte Carlo: 3.138–3.148 ≈ π).

**Argument 4 ("exactly 4" = Clifford interior space)【Structural argument】**: regular-tetrahedron 4 directions = interior-space component structure (Clifford Cl(1,3) representation dimension 4 = two time directions × two chirality directions); octahedron 6 directions = exterior-space axis structure (3 axes ±). Entropy encoding uses interior-space components ⟹ direction-cluster number 4. The three candidates (four quadrants / Hopf fibration / SU(2) double cover) unify in the same "4".

**Proposition 5 (1/4 coefficient)【Derivation】**: Theorem 1 (k≥4) → Theorem 2 (regular tetrahedron) → Theorem 3 (Voronoi π/cell) → Argument 4 (4=Clifford) → Nyquist (coherent cross-section 4l_P²) → **1 nat** per unit (**round-39 unit correction**: it read "1 bit", with the conversion left dangling in the factor ln2 on the right) ⟹ **S/k_B = A/(4l_P²) (nats)**, cross-validated with the §4.8 microstate counting as **two paths**.

**Proposition 6 (interior-space encoding priority)【Derivation, framework-internal deduction】**: the final step of Argument 4 — why entropy encoding uses the interior-space 4 components rather than the exterior-space 3 axes (6-direction octahedron). Three-lemma chain:
- Lemma A (exterior-space entropy = 0): isotropy (Axiom 1) ⟹ no information in directional preference (uniform prior, numerically ‖⟨n⟩‖≈0); no-hair【Framework assumption: no-hair】⟹ exterior structure erased; **event point-like-ness【Assumption H-8: event point-like-ness】 "two vortices dragged in different directions cannot coexist in the same micro-variation event" ⟹ directions are not intra-event attributes**;
- Lemma B (interior-space non-zero): quantum-line endpoints carry Clifford 4-component states latched across branches【Framework assumption】; Hawking radiation = leakage of interior-space states ⟹ the interior space is the information carrier;
    ⚠️ **【v2.9 supplement (round 55: the cross-branch channel carries *information*, not *energy*)】** Lemma B fixes the
    purpose of the cross-branch channel as **information** (Clifford 4-component state latching). This round **adds an explicit
    boundary** so that it cannot later be misused as an energy / matter conduit:

      energy / matter conduit within this framework.**

## 5 Gauge-Group Structure

### 5.1 "Pairing order" and the three gauge groups

【Derivation】 Define the **pairing order** n = the smallest positive integer such that the representation tensor product ⊗ⁿ contains the trivial representation:

| Group | n | Representation theory | Physics |
|---|---|---|---|
| U(1) | 1 | all one-dimensional, q⊗(−q)=1 | long-range, massless photon |
| SU(2)_L | 2 | 2 pseudo-real (2≅2̄, ε^ij sign-preserving) | short-range (massive W/Z), chiral |
| SU(3)_c | 3 | 3 complex (3≇3̄), ε_ijk third-order | color confinement (qqq cube has no solution) |

**Representation-theoretic root of color confinement**: colorless combinations of baryons qqq require the third-order antisymmetric invariant tensor ε_ijk — three quarks form a singlet via the third-order tensor. Mesons qq̄ use δ_i^j (n=2 structure).

**【v2.5 definition revision (referee M6)】**: the pairing order is precisely defined as **n ≡ d_min(G)** (smallest non-trivial irreducible representation dimension = number of color components on string-net edges), supplemented by the **same-sign pairing order n′** (smallest n such that ρ⊗ⁿ without conjugation contains the trivial representation): U(1) n′=∞ (same-sign never contains trivial ⟹ **must pair positive–anti = representation-theoretic root of charge conjugation C**), SU(2) n′=2 (2⊗2=3⊕1, pseudo-real), SU(3) n′=3 (3⊗3⊗3=10⊕8⊕8⊕1, ε_ijk). **Structural necessity: non-Abelian-ness ⟺ n′ finite ⟺ same-sign tensor products can form singlets** (see §5.2 screening argument).

### 5.2 Screening argument (why SU(3)×SU(2)×U(1) — structural screening)

**【v2.5 structural screening (referee M6)】**: Given the pairing order n, the candidate group pool (U(1)/Z_N/SU(2)/Q₈/S₃/SO(3)/SU(3)/G₂/SU(4)/Sp(2), 10 total) is eliminated one by one by three criteria, with a **unique survivor at each step** (numerical verification in the M6 report, dimension conservation all ✓):

- **S1 dimension matching**: d_min(G) = n (n = vortex winding-mode number, §5.3 input)
- **S2 same-sign pairing**: n′(G) = n (non-Abelian-ness ⟺ n′ finite)
- **S3 third-order antisymmetry**: n=3 requires ε_ijk (det=1 ⟹ unique to the SU(N) sequence)

| n | Candidates (d_min matching) | Reason excluded | Unique survivor |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | U(1), Z_N | Z_N discrete, Villain limit = U(1) (v1.30 Theorem A generalized) | **U(1)** (unique 1D connected compact Abelian Lie group) |
| 2 | SU(2), Q₈, Sp(2), S₃ | Q₈ ⊂ SU(2) discrete; Sp(2) symplectic pseudo-real (chirality picks SU(2)); S₃ same class (d_min=2) | **SU(2)** (2 pseudo-real → chiral) |
| 3 | SU(3), SO(3), G₂, SU(4), A₄/S₄/Δ(27) | **SO(3) n′=2 (3 real self-dual → 3⊗3 contains trivial → no confinement forcing)**; G₂ d_min=7; SU(4) n=4; A₄ etc. are SU(3) discrete subgroups | **SU(3)** (3 complex 3≇3̄ + ε_ijk → qqq third-order singlet) |

**Decisive exclusion (SO(3))**: although d_min(SO(3))=3, its 3-dimensional representation is real self-dual (3≅3̄) → same-sign pairing order n′=2 ≠ 3 (3⊗3=5⊕3⊕1 already contains trivial) → **structurally incapable of third-order confinement** — a direct answer to "why SU(3) rather than SO(3)".

**n≥4 exclusion**: three spatial dimensions (locked by the Kakeya dimension) ⟹ the fourth-order antisymmetric ε_ijkl requires 4-dimensional space; n=4 Fermat has no solution (original argument retained); SU(2)×U(1) cannot merge (chirality, original argument retained).

**Discrete–continuum bridge (correcting the crude A2 mapping)**: the framework's numerical layer uses quantum-double D(G) discrete groups — Z_N⊂U(1), **Q₈⊂SU(2)** (i²=j²=k²=−I), **S₃⊂SU(3)** (even-permutation matrices, det=1) — with unique continuous parent groups; **S₃ itself has d_min=2 and belongs to the n=2 class; it is used as an SU(3) permutation subgroup for non-Abelian numerical verification (v1.33)**.

**Residual contingency localized**: after screening uniqueness, the only remaining input = the micro-origin of the vortex winding-mode numbers n=1,2,3 (§5.3 assumption, not derivable from representation theory — honestly retained).

### 5.3 Three fermion generations = vortex winding modes

## 6 Dynamics (Least Action)

### 6.1 Action functional

【Derivation】 Minimal Lagrangian density (multi-representation version):

$$\mathcal{L} = \sqrt{-g}\Big[\frac{1}{16\pi G}R - \tfrac14 F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu} - \tfrac12(\partial\Phi)^2 - V(\Phi) + i\bar\psi\gamma^\mu\nabla_\mu\psi - m\bar\psi\psi - g\Phi\bar\psi\psi - eA_\mu\bar\psi\gamma^\mu\psi\Big]$$

- Pinning term gΦψ̄ψ: environment-dependent effective mass m_eff(x) = m + gΦ(x) — "the particle is pinned by the storm";
- Φ is the condensate representative of annihilation–regeneration noise (auxiliary field).

### 6.2 Field equations (variational derivation)

| Channel | Field equation | Correspondence |
|---|---|---|
| scalar | □Φ = V′(Φ) + gψ̄ψ | **concrete realization of D[Φ]=S** |
| vector | ∂_μF^μν = eJ^ν | Maxwell |
| tensor | G_μν = 8πG T_μν | Einstein |

**Degeneracy checks**: g=0, V=½m²Φ² → free Dirac + scalar + GR; ψ=0, Φ=const → GR + cosmological constant; weak-field low-velocity → g₀₀ ≈ −(1+2Φ_N/c²).

### 6.3 Noether conservation laws

【Derivation】 time translation → energy, spatial translation → momentum, rotation → angular momentum, internal U(1) → charge (4 conservation laws); local U(1) absent (single scalar without A_μ; completed in the multi-representation version).

### 6.4 Spontaneous symmetry breaking (Φ⁴ thermal transition)

$$V(\Phi) = -\tfrac12\mu^2\Phi^2 + \tfrac{\lambda}{4}\Phi^4,\qquad V_T = V(\Phi) + \tfrac12 cT^2\Phi^2$$

- T > T_c = μ/√c: ⟨Φ⟩=0 (unified phase); T < T_c: Mexican hat (broken);
- complex-scalar upgrade → Anderson–Higgs → W/Z masses = "draft-pipe collective mode".

**Physical mapping**: "Higgs = flux-field phase transition" ⟺ order parameter ⟨Φ⟩ rising from 0 to v; "breaking = mode-boundary emergence" ⟺ temperature (flux density) dropping below T_c.

### 6.5 Geodesic equation

【Citation】 Variating the point-particle action S_pp = −m∫dτ gives the Γ equation; Newtonian limit a⃗ = −∇Φ_N. "The body is readjusted at each instant to minimize the pressure difference" ⟺ Φ_N = flux negative-pressure potential.

## 7 Foundations of Quantum Mechanics

### 7.1 The problem: classical event flow cannot yield Bell violation

【Citation + Derivation】 A classical hidden-variable flow field can only give CHSH ≤ 2, contradicting the experimental CHSH = 2√2 — **the framework must be non-commutativized**. The original work was already aware of this ("for this to survive, you must be able to derive 2√2 … a structural product of source events").

### 7.2 Non-commutative event flow

【Derivation】**Proposition (non-commutative event flow ⇒ quantum correlations)**: upgrade the event-flow state from a classical probability distribution (commutative algebra L^∞) to an operator algebra (C* algebra), with "encounter–annihilation" events corresponding to non-commuting projection operators (Clifford-type {Π_μ, Π_ν}≠0). Then:
(i) joint measurement of entangled pairs gives CHSH = 2√2 (Tsirelson bound);
(ii) complementarity V² + I² ≤ 1 holds automatically;
(iii) uncertainty ΔA·ΔB ≥ ½|⟨[A,B]⟩| holds automatically.

**Non-commutative elements already in the framework**: Clifford algebra (micro-variations do not commute), path integral (−1)^N_exchange (exchange sign), geometric origin of superposition states (global equivalence of the centers of positive/anti storms), quantum lines (events of common origin).

**Numerical verification**: Bell state |ψ⁻⟩ + optimal measurement basis ⟹ S = −2.8284 = −2√2 ✓; path-state inner product gives V²+I² = 1 ✓.

### 7.3 Quantum walk: dynamics of the non-commutative event flow

【Derivation】 Discrete-time quantum walk U = S(C⊗I): coin flip C (Hadamard, non-commutative) + conditional shift S. Numerics: quantum σ ~ t (ballistic) vs classical √t (diffusive) — non-commutativization preserves coherence.

### 7.4 Positioning clarification

【Derivation】 Once non-commutativized, the framework's quantum part **mathematically coincides** with standard QM. The framework's unique contribution = providing an **ontological motivation for "why quantum mechanics"** (in the spirit of Gel'fand–Naimark: commutative C* ⟺ classical probability; non-commutative C* ⟺ quantum states). The positioning is **'t Hooft cellular-automaton-like**: an event flow that is **deterministic given the tick sequence** (each tick is a permutation ⟹ bijective, reversible, zero entropy production, §8.6.7), with a non-commutative observable algebra. ⚠️ This does **not** claim global determinism — the tick sequence itself is an exogenous input, and the provenance of its randomness is undecidable within the framework (see the adjudication record in the round-57 block below).

### 8.3 Dark-matter superposition model

【Derivation】**Dark matter = this-branch Planck-fluctuation remnants (ε_B mechanism) + mirror-branch gravitational projection (cross-branch mechanism)**:

$$\rho_{DM} = \varepsilon_B\,\rho_{raw}\,f_{space} + \kappa_{mirror}\,\rho_{mirror}$$

- Mirror-branch projection: **【v2.9 correction: falsified】** the earlier "same order as observation" claim fails. The two natural readings of 50:50 give opposite-direction discrepancies:
  reading A (only gravity shared across branches, the rest filtered ⟹ Ω_dm = Ω_b = 0.0493) is **5.31× too low**;
  reading B (keeping κ_extra ≈ Ω_mirror/Ω_total ≈ 0.5 ⟹ Ω_dm ~ 1) is **3.82× too high** — both far outside the error bar.
  ⟹ The claim that the mirror branch supplies the **bulk** of dark matter is withdrawn; this subsection is downgraded to an open problem, and the void-CPT-κ anomaly holds only if the mechanism is rebuilt.
- Testable prediction: **void-CPT-κ anomaly** (void regions with stronger weak lensing + mass–light decoupling, most prominent at 10–30 Mpc/h scales; candidate objects CDG-2, J0613+52).

### 8.4 Dark energy and w(z)

【Derivation】 Dark energy = contribution of the ε_A saturation region ρ_Λ ~ (E_c)⁴; the saturation scale drifts slowly with cosmic expansion E_c(z) = E_c,0(1+z)^ε ⟹

$$w = -1 + \frac{4\varepsilon}{3} = -1 + 2\nu$$

Observational constraint (DESI 2024+CMB: w = −1.028±0.031): |ε| < 0.023, |ν| < 0.0155. **The framework predicts w = const (wa=0)** — directly testable with DESI DR2/Euclid. **【v2.7 supplement: DESI DR2 (2025-03) discrimination status (referee M5)】**: DESI DR2+CMB exclude ΛCDM at **3.1σ** (+Pantheon+ 2.8σ / +Union3 3.8σ / +DESY5 4.2σ), with CPL best fit **w₀≈−0.76±0.06, wa≈−0.77±0.23** — exerting **~3.3σ pressure on the framework's wa=0 prediction** (the framework's dark-energy prediction is being discriminated by **existing data**: if the DDE signal is confirmed ⟹ this prediction is falsified; if a systematic effect ⟹ the framework survives). **Verdict timeline**: DESI final analysis 2026-04 + Euclid first cosmological data 2026-10 — **final verdict within 0-2 years**. Honest note: the DDE-vs-systematics debate is unresolved (ACT DR6/SPT-3G CMB support ΛCDM); the w₀ discrimination is a cross-model wCDM/CPL comparison (not directly comparable; the wa=0 test is authoritative).
